<!-- gear-plan project=gear base=774 -->
# Goal
Do all the work aligned for M5 and deliver a complete new version that never stops: no hand fix during the whole voyage, no lane idle while there is work it could start, and a board that matches the agreed prototype. Released at each station: S1 reliability fixes and conventions as configuration (v0.5.0); S2 the small-task procedure and each role's place in the skill (v0.5.1); S3 goal, bar and stations locked (v0.5.2); S4 the board built to its prototype, then the final release (v0.5.3).

# Milestone M5: a complete version that never stops
done: (1) every M5 task merged; (2) the voyage never stops: while some lane has a task it can start, no lane sits idle; (3) every hand fix the captain makes during M5 is either turned into an M5 task and merged, or, only when it needs a sweeping change, written into gear/voyages/M6/backlog.md; (4) the board matches the prototype in gear/voyages/M5/board/: every view passes its golden-frame test and the user has checked it view by view; (5) released at each station, the last as v0.5.x
bar: DELIVERABLE: an error in normal use blocks the task; edge cases outside normal use are logged
eta: 21h

## T-403 [now] lane=mast milestone=M5 required
goal: [v0.5.0 S1] The board never flashes: each frame overwrites the last in place (2026-10-29: the board clears the whole screen and redraws every 2-3 s, so the page blinks). Cause, crates/gear-board/src/screen.rs fn draw: it writes `\x1b[H\x1b[2J` (home and clear the whole screen) before every frame. Change crates/gear-board/src/screen.rs and the two callers in crates/gear-board/src/lib.rs only, no other design: (1) fn draw writes `\x1b[?2026h` (begin synchronized update), `\x1b[H`, the frame with `\x1b[K` (erase to end of line) before each newline and after the last line, then `\x1b[J` (erase below) and `\x1b[?2026l` (end synchronized update), and never `\x1b[2J`; (2) the run loop keeps the last frame it wrote and skips the write when the new frame text is identical, except after a resize, when it draws once with a full clear. The code change is in those files only; tests go where the crate keeps them.
accept: screen.rs tests: draw's output starts with `\x1b[?2026h\x1b[H`, holds no `\x1b[2J`, puts `\x1b[K` before every newline and ends with `\x1b[J\x1b[?2026l`; a board test: two refreshes with unchanged data write the frame once; a resize writes it again. Run only `make check CRATE=gear-board`; the merge gate runs `make gate`.

## T-391 [now] lane=hull milestone=M5 required
goal: [v0.5.0 S1] A reviewer started for a pending review is never released as idle before it gets that review (another project, 2026-10-29: 844 temp-seat starts in one voyage of about fifty reviews, generations 361, 173, 120, 98, 67, 25 by seat number; review-4 seen as `released` / "idle reviewer released" while `engine.review_pending.<seat>` still held T-150, then started again, about every 20 s, each start a login shell and a codex launch). Cause, crates/gear-engine/src/release.rs fn release_idle_standby: it treats every Standby temp as jobless ("A standby reviewer has no job"), but a seat started for a pending review sits in Standby until its agent is idle and fn start (review.rs) assigns the review; when the Standby seats outnumber the lanes with a Started attempt, the sweep releases it, the pending review starts it again, and the loop repeats. Change fn release_idle_standby only, no other design: a Standby seat whose `engine.review_pending.<seat id>` is non-empty is left out of the sweep (it has a job; the existing under_review check in fn release_reviewers still ends a reservation whose review is gone), and the excess is counted over the seats left in. The code change is in crates/gear-engine/src/release.rs only; tests go where the crate keeps them.
accept: release.rs tests: with no lane working (W = 0), a Standby temp with `engine.review_pending` = T-1 (T-1 Verifying, attempt 1 Submitted) is not released and keeps its pending, while a Standby temp with no pending is released as today; with W = 1 and two Standby temps of which one has a pending, the one without a pending stays (excess 0); the existing release tests pass. Run only `make check CRATE=gear-engine TESTS='<a nextest filter: the tests of the module you changed and your new test>'`; the merge gate runs `make gate`.

## T-390 [now] lane=mast milestone=M5 required
goal: [v0.5.0 S1] The voyage forecast measures each lane by its throughput, so a station's pace and projected finish are true (2026-10-29: at 488 min the station said pace 0.98x and finish 1248 min, while the lanes had done 2265 estimated minutes in 488: hull 755 at 0.65 min per estimated minute, mast 1510 at 0.32, a finish near 845 min). Cause, crates/gear-board/src/voyage.rs fn build_reports: a lane's `done_min` is the sum of its done tasks' spans from the first attempt's `started` to the last step (`merged`), and review and merge run while the lane is already on its next task, so the same minutes are counted twice or more. Change fn build_reports only, no other design: a lane's `done_min` becomes the minutes from the earliest `started` of any attempt of the lane's voyage tasks to the latest `handed_in` of the attempts of its done tasks (a done task with no `handed_in` uses its last step); a lane with no done task keeps `done_min` 0 and no pace as today; `r.measured_actual_min` is the sum of the lanes' `done_min`; each task's own span still sets `slowest`. The code change is in crates/gear-board/src/voyage.rs only; tests go where the crates keep them, and the existing expectations in crates/gear-board and crates/gear-app/tests/it (voyage_contract.rs, station_contract.rs, workload_contract.rs) that encode the old sum of spans are updated to the new measure, each named in the hand-in.
accept: A voyage test: one lane, two done tasks of estimate 30, task 1 started at 0, handed in at 20, merged at 40, task 2 started at 20, handed in at 40, merged at 60 → the lane's `done_min` is 40 and its pace 0.67, the voyage pace 0.67 (today 100/60); a second lane with no done task has no pace and the finish stays unknown as today. Run only `make check CRATE=gear-board` and `make check CRATE=gear-app`; the merge gate runs `make gate`.
estimate: 30

## T-392 [must] lane=mast milestone=M5 required
goal: [v0.5.0 S1] A departed voyage's staffing cannot be changed by a re-init without the user's words. The staffing is the config's `quota`, `lanes`, `temps` and `models` sections, agreed at kickoff like the goal (T-331 locks the goal the same way). Change, no other design: (1) crates/gear-app/src/admin.rs fn re_register: when the project has a voyage with a departure and a task that is not terminal (`tx.voyages_of_project`, `tx.tasks_of_project`), parse the stored `project.config_toml` (fn config::parse) and compare those four sections with the new config (derive `PartialEq` on their types in crates/gear-app/src/config.rs where missing); when any differs and no user words are given, refuse with a new `ErrorCode::StaffLocked` (crates/gear-model, next to the existing codes) and the text "the staffing of voyage <M> was agreed at its kickoff: <each changed field as `section.key: old → new`, `; `-joined> — ask the user first, then run init again with --user \"<the user's words>\"", and write nothing; with user words, apply as today and record one starred decision in that voyage through `tx.insert_decision_if_new` (as plan.rs fn record_gear_decision builds it, but `star: true`, kind "staff", what the changed fields as in the refusal, why "user: «<words>»"). `init_with_home` and `init` take the words as `user: Option<&str>`. (2) crates/gear-cli/src/main.rs `AdminOp::Init` gains `#[arg(long)] user: Option<String>` passed through; `--check` reports the same refusal text as a problem without writing. (3) skills/gear/references/captain.md, the paragraph on re-running init mid-voyage: one sentence: "Staffing (`[quota]`, `[[lanes]]`, `[temps]`, `[models]`) is agreed at kickoff; mid-voyage init refuses a change to it until you pass the user's own words with `--user`." The code change is in crates/gear-app/src/admin.rs, crates/gear-app/src/config.rs, crates/gear-model (the error code), crates/gear-cli/src/main.rs and that skill file only (allowed edits for mast); tests go where the crates keep them.
accept: gear-app tests (crates/gear-app/tests/it, the init fixtures): with a departed voyage and an open task, a re-init that changes `quota.temps` from 6 to 3 is refused with StaffLocked, its text holds `quota.temps: 6 → 3`, and the stored config version is unchanged; the same re-init with user words applies, the version rises by one, and the voyage holds one starred decision of kind "staff" whose why holds the words; a change outside the four sections (e.g. `conventions.merge_message`) applies without words; with no departed voyage every change applies as today. A CLI test: `gear admin init --check` on such a change prints the refusal text. Run only `make check CRATE=gear-app` and `make check CRATE=gear-cli`; the merge gate runs `make gate`.

## T-394 [now] lane=mast milestone=M5 required
goal: [v0.5.0 S1] A stop the user asked for stays stopped. Cause, crates/gear-cli/src/main.rs: every gear call runs fn ensure_engine, which starts an engine whenever none holds the lock, so the next call by any lane, reviewer or board restarts it seconds after `gear admin stop`. The captain's hand fix was to comment out the session's line in ~/.config/gear/targets.tsv. Change crates/gear-cli/src/main.rs only, no other design: (1) `AdminOp::Stop` gains `#[arg(long)] hold: bool`; with it, after the stop (or when no engine runs) the CLI writes the file `engine.hold` in the engine dir (fn engine_dir) holding the time and "held by gear admin stop --hold", and prints "engine held: no gear call starts it until `gear admin start`". (2) fn ensure_engine returns without starting when `engine.hold` exists, and prints to stderr once per call "gear: the engine is held (gear admin stop --hold); `gear admin start` resumes it". (3) A new `AdminOp::Start` removes `engine.hold` and starts the engine as fn ensure_engine does, printing the pid; with no hold it only starts. Agents cannot run either (the existing ctx check of cmd_admin_stop). (4) fn stop_notice's text (T-298) is unchanged. The code change is in crates/gear-cli/src/main.rs only; tests go where the crate keeps them.
accept: CLI tests over a scratch engine dir: `stop --hold` with no engine running writes engine.hold and prints the held line; fn ensure_engine with the hold present starts nothing and prints the held line; `start` removes the hold; a plain `stop` writes no hold. Run only `make check CRATE=gear-cli`; the merge gate runs `make gate`.

## T-396 [wont] lane=hull milestone=M5 required
goal: [v0.5.0 S1] P-1 for another project (its user stopped its voyage over it twice): a reviewer started a moment ago is never released as "agent gone" before its agent can show up, so a review seat is not started and released every tick (2026-10-29: its review-2 cycled every 8-25 s, ops O-1555..O-1569 voided "the seat is not reachable (released)", generation 383, a new pane each time). Cause, crates/gear-engine/src/release.rs fn release_finished_reviews, the T-264 loop at its top (lines 65-101): it runs in the same tick as review.rs fn start_reviews (lib.rs: start at :498, release at :523, ops at :551); for the seat just started, Active with a task and an Active binding, `herdr.agent_get(pane)` returns `Ok(None)` because the new codex agent is not reported yet, so the loop voids the binding, clears the task, sets Releasing ("its agent is gone mid-review") and closes the pane; the next loop releases it (reason "idle reviewer released", since its task was just cleared), its Deliver op is voided, and the next tick starts it again. Change crates/gear-engine/src/release.rs only, no other design: (1) in that T-264 loop, skip a seat whose Active binding was created less than 60 s ago (`now - binding.created < 60`): it keeps its slot and its op; (2) count each "agent gone mid-review" release per task and attempt in the meta key `engine.review_agent_gone.<project>.<task>.<attempt>`; on the third, instead of releasing, set the seat NeedsCheck with the reason "the reviewer's agent was gone right after its start 3 times for <task> attempt <n>" and put one action item in the captain's inbox on thread `review:<task>:agent-gone` with that text (as the crate's other captain items are written), so a CLI that really dies at launch stops instead of looping; (3) fn release_one keeps the reason a seat already carries when it arrives Releasing with no task, instead of "idle reviewer released". The code change is in crates/gear-engine/src/release.rs only; tests go where the crate keeps them.
accept: Engine tests with FakeHerdr (a project with `[temps]`, a Released temp seat and a Submitted hand-in): the fake's agent_start succeeds but agent_get of the new pane returns None for the first tick: after that tick the seat is Active, its Deliver op is not void, and `reviewers` holds the seat while `released` does not; when agent_get stays None past 60 s on three starts of the same task and attempt, the seat ends NeedsCheck with the reason above and one captain item, not a fourth start; a seat Releasing with no task keeps its reason; the existing release and review tests pass. Run only `make check CRATE=gear-engine`; the merge gate runs `make gate`.

## T-400 [must] lane=hull milestone=M5 required
goal: [v0.5.0 S1] Changing a lane's model mid-voyage is documented and proven, including a lane that was held (another project, 2026-10-29: its captain changed two lanes' models by init, then held one lane, exited its agent and released it; it came back on the earlier model, because the hold took it out of the config reconciliation). What exists: crates/gear-engine/src/restart.rs fn reconcile_config restarts an Active or Standby lane onto the registered config's chain head when it differs from the last applied one (meta `engine.restart_config.<seat>`), and delivers "Continue T-<n>" when an attempt is open; a Held seat is skipped. Change, no other design: (1) restart.rs fn reconcile_config: a seat that is not Active or Standby keeps its stored `engine.restart_config` untouched (today's skip), and when such a seat is next Active or Standby the comparison with the registered chain runs as for any other seat, so a change made while it was held is applied then; add a test for that sequence (Held, re-init to another model, release with the agent alive → restarted on the new model). (2) An engine test: a re-init that changes a lane's model while its attempt is Started restarts the lane on the new model (engine.model.<seat> is the new one) and delivers "Continue T-<n>". (3) skills/gear/references/conventions.md, after the `[[lanes]]` example: a lane may run its own model and CLI with `models = [{ model = "<id>", cli = "<pi|codex|opencode|claude>" }]` in its `[[lanes]]` block, which overrides `[models] lanes` for that lane (e.g. two lanes on one model, one on another, one on codex); and skills/gear/references/captain.md, a short subsection "Changing a lane's model mid-voyage": change the model or CLI only when the user asks and has confirmed it in a dialog; re-init with the new chain (--confirmed after departure); leave the lane Active: the engine restarts it on the new model and it continues its task; check `engine.model.<seat>`; never hold, kill or release a lane for this. The code change is in crates/gear-engine/src/restart.rs, skills/gear/references/conventions.md and skills/gear/references/captain.md only; tests go where the crate keeps them.

## T-401 [must] lane=mast milestone=M5 required
goal: [v0.5.0 S1] A staffing change after departure is authorized by a confirmation, not by pasted words: T-392 made `gear admin init` refuse it without `--user "<the user's words>"`, and GEAR is not to store the user's words. A sensitive change (goal, bar, staffing) happens only when the user asks for it, and then the captain confirms it with the user in a dialog; at every other time the captain asks nothing and changes none of them. Change, no other design: (1) crates/gear-cli/src/main.rs `AdminOp::Init`: `--user <words>` becomes `#[arg(long)] confirmed: bool` ("the user asked for this staffing change and confirmed it"), passed through as `confirmed: bool` in place of `user: Option<&str>`; (2) crates/gear-app/src/admin.rs: the refusal text ends "— change staffing only when the user asks; confirm it with them, then run init again with --confirmed", and the starred staff decision's why is "confirmed by the user" (no words stored; the blank-words check of T-392's rework goes); (3) skills/gear/references/captain.md: T-392's sentence becomes "Staffing (`[quota]`, `[[lanes]]`, `[temps]`, `[models]`) is agreed at kickoff; mid-voyage init refuses a change to it until you pass --confirmed.", and one rule in the section on talking to the user: "Ask the user to confirm (a dialog) only when they ask you to change the goal, the bar or the staffing; never ask anything otherwise, and never change those on your own." The code change is in crates/gear-cli/src/main.rs, crates/gear-app/src/admin.rs and skills/gear/references/captain.md only; tests go where the crates keep them.
accept: (Moved to mast for balance at station M-5422: the files this task names are allowed edits for mast.) gear-app tests: the T-392 staffing tests run with `confirmed: true` in place of user words and the decision's why is "confirmed by the user"; without it the refusal text ends with the --confirmed sentence. A CLI test: `gear admin init --confirmed` parses and `--user` is gone. Run only `make check CRATE=gear-app` and `make check CRATE=gear-cli`; the merge gate runs `make gate`.

## T-413 [now] lane=hull milestone=M5 required
goal: [v0.5.0 S1] Every seat GEAR starts writes its temp files to its own scratch directory, never to the system disk (2026-10-29: lanes, reviewers and tests filled the system disk to 1 GB free through /tmp and the system temp directory; the user requires a lasting, configurable answer, as Claude Code's own temp directory is configurable). Change, no other design: (1) crates/gear-app/src/config.rs `Conventions` gains `scratch_dir: Option<String>` read from `[conventions] scratch_dir` (a path; `~` expanded); (2) crates/gear-engine/src/onboard.rs, where `env_command` is built: the seat's scratch directory is `<scratch_dir>/<project>/<seat name>` when set, else `<the seat's worktree>/.gear/tmp`; the line becomes `mkdir -p <scratch>; export TMPDIR=<scratch>; export GEAR_CTX=…; export GEAR_DB=…` (TMPDIR is what pi, codex, claude, cargo and the OS temp API read); a worktree default also appends `.gear/` once to the repository's common `info/exclude` (`git rev-parse --git-common-dir`) when it is not listed; (3) crates/gear-engine/src/release.rs fn release_one, after a seat is released or retired: its scratch directory is removed (`std::fs::remove_dir_all`, errors ignored); (4) skills/gear/references/conventions.md lists `scratch_dir` with the default, and skills/gear/references/rules/lane.md and rules/review.md say "Temp files and scratch copies go in $TMPDIR (GEAR sets it to your own scratch directory), never /tmp." (replacing T-412's line if present). The code change is in these files only; tests go where each crate keeps them.
accept: Engine tests: an onboarded lane's env line starts with `mkdir -p <worktree>/.gear/tmp; export TMPDIR=<worktree>/.gear/tmp;`; with `scratch_dir = "/data/scratch"` it is `/data/scratch/<project>/<seat>`; `.gear/` is added to info/exclude once; a released seat's scratch directory is gone. Config tests: `scratch_dir` parses and defaults to none. Run only `make check CRATE=gear-engine` and `make check CRATE=gear-app`; the merge gate runs `make gate`.

## T-361 [must] lane=hull milestone=M5 required
goal: [v0.5.3 S4] The rows of the git-style task graph are computed from the store, as the prototype's fn view_graph computes them (README change 8; prototype.py lines 384-426: dev is the main line, newest on top; each task a branch that leaves dev where its work starts and joins it at its merge; a task not started hangs dashed in its lane). This task builds the rows; T-362 paints them. Change crates/gear-board/src/graph.rs only, no other design: (1) `GraphTask` gains `pub rank: i64` and `pub started: Option<Ts>` (the earliest `started` of its attempts). (2) `pub struct GraphRow { pub cells: Vec<(usize, char, Style)>, pub task: Option<String>, pub text: String, pub style: Style }` and `pub fn git_rows(tasks: &[GraphTask], lanes: &[String], departure: Ts, width: usize) -> Vec<GraphRow>`, with the tasks taken in rank order (T), minutes = (ts − departure)/60, col(lane) = 9 + 6·(its index in `lanes`), info_x = col(last lane) + 6, tw = max(30, min(56, width − info_x − 52)), lane style = `Style::fg(lane_color(lanes, lane))`; info(t) = id padded to 7 + title cut to tw padded to tw + two spaces + `words(t)` cut to 36 padded to 36 + time, time = "<actual>/<est>m" when actual > 0 else "<est>m", plus " · <rounds> rounds" when rounds > 1; waiting = the tasks with no `started` and stage not Moved; open = the tasks with `started` and no `finished`; live = open (a set); verticals(skip) = for each task of T in live but skip: (col(its lane), '│', its lane style). Rows: for each waiting task, the last to run first (reverse rank, so the next task to run sits just above the running ones and the graph reads newest on top like git): (verticals(none) + (col, '○', lane style bold), task, info, Dim) then (verticals(none) + (col, '┆', lane style), task, "", plain); events = (started minutes, 0, t) and (finished minutes, 1, t), sorted by (minutes, kind) from the largest down, equal keys keeping T order (a stable sort); for a merge event: (verticals(t) + (3, '●', Ok bold) + (x, '─', lane style) for x in 4..col + (col, '╯', lane style), task, info + "  merged <graph clock of finished>", plain), then t joins live; for a start event: when t is open, first (verticals(t) + (col, t's `symbol()`, `color()` bold), task, info, Bold); then (verticals(t) + (3, '├', Ok) + (x, '─', lane style) for x in 4..col + (col, '╮', lane style), None, 7 spaces + "starts +<hm(minutes)>" + " after <deps joined by ", ">" when it has deps, Dim), and t leaves live. `hm` is "<h>h<mm>m" with hours always. The code change is in crates/gear-board/src/graph.rs only; tests go where the crate keeps them.
accept: Graph unit tests on GraphTasks equal to the prototype's M5 voyage (WHEN's minutes, departure 0): 24 rows; the first is T-265's "○" row with text starting "T-265  goal-change line with the user's words" and "waits for T-262 to merge"; the eighth row (index 7) has '├' at column 3, '─' at 4-14 and '╮' at 15, no task, and the text "       starts +2h09m after T-261"; the last two rows are T-257's and T-258's forks, both "       starts +0h05m" (equal minutes keep plan order). Run only `make check CRATE=gear-board`; the merge gate runs `make gate`.
estimate: 45

## T-381 [must] lane=hull milestone=M5 required
goal: [v0.5.0 S1] A review round ends when its attempt is settled by anyone, so a settled task's overrun alert never comes back (another project, 2026-10-29: M-844 T-117, M-906 T-88, M-1074 T-104 fired as urgent "budget 10 min; active 196 min; no answer" again and again for four hours after the captain settled each in # Verdicts; their state shows `engine.overrun.<p>.T-88.2.review` with asked_active 11 203 s and no_answer true). Cause, crates/gear-engine/src/overrun.rs fn process: a review round counts as live while it has no reviewer verdict (`get_review_verdict`) and no `end_at`; a captain's verdict writes neither, so the round stays live for ever, its active time grows, and the "no answer" item is reopened every 15 minutes. Change fn process only, no other design: a review round is live only when, besides today's two conditions, the round's attempt is still `AttemptState::Submitted` and its task `TaskState::Verifying`; when it is not live for that reason, its stop is the attempt's settle time (`tx.attempt_times(…).settled` when that field exists, else `now`), and its open overrun item on thread `overrun:<task>:review` for that attempt is set Resolved with "the attempt was settled" once. The code change is in overrun.rs only; tests go where the crate keeps them.
accept: overrun.rs tests: a review round whose attempt a captain's fail verdict settled (no review verdict, no end_at) asks nothing and writes no item on later ticks, and an open overrun item of it is resolved once; a live review round of a Submitted attempt still asks at its budget as today; the existing overrun tests pass. Run only `make check CRATE=gear-engine`; the merge gate runs `make gate`.

## T-379 [must] lane=hull milestone=M5 required

Today crates/gear-store/src/lib.rs waits `BUSY_TIMEOUT_MS` = 5 000 ms on a lock and then fails, and nothing says which transaction held it. Change crates/gear-store/src/lib.rs only, no other design: (1) `BUSY_TIMEOUT_MS` becomes 30 000; (2) fn write measures the time from its `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` to the commit or rollback with `std::time::Instant`, and when it is over 1 000 ms writes one line to stderr, `gear-store: a write held the store for <ms> ms (<the caller's location>)`, the location taken with `#[track_caller]` on fn write and `std::panic::Location::caller()`; the engine's stderr is its log, so the slow writer shows there. Nothing else changes. The code change is in crates/gear-store/src/lib.rs only; tests go where the crate keeps them.
accept: Store tests: `BUSY_TIMEOUT_MS` is 30 000; a second connection whose write starts while the first holds a write for 200 ms succeeds after it (no Locked); a helper that formats the slow-write line names the caller's file and line. No test sleeps beyond that 200 ms hold. Run only `make check CRATE=gear-store`; the merge gate runs `make gate`.

(4) `crate::tasks::VerdictHint` gains `plan: String`, filled in fn verdict_hint with `layout::plan_file(&conventions.voyage_dir, project, milestone)` from the project's stored config (the default `Conventions` when it does not parse; `<M>` for a task without milestone); fn verdict_command and the station hint use that path instead of `gear/voyages/{milestone}.md`; the CLI error says "give the voyage file, e.g. gear plan apply gear/voyages/M1/plan.md". (6) skills/gear/references/captain.md, conventions.md, github.md and README.md: the voyage file is `gear/voyages/<M>/plan.md` (one folder per voyage, with its reports/ and reviews/), and conventions.md lists `remote` and `voyage_dir` with their defaults beside the branch templates. A job whose attempt is still Started behaves exactly as today.
accept: Engine tests in review.rs, with the fixtures of `a_hand_in_takes_the_seat_a_direction_result_frees`: a running direction job whose attempt is handed in with no result is closed by finish_direction_results, its seat goes Standby with no task, and the hand-in's review starts on the next start_reviews pass; a failing direction result that arrives after the hand-in writes no message to the lane and one Info message to the captain; a running job on a Started attempt with no result stays running. Existing direction tests pass. Run only `make check CRATE=gear-engine`; the merge gate runs `make gate`.

## T-265 [must] lane=mast milestone=M5 required
goal: [v0.5.0 S1] `gear admin stop` never cuts a merge in half (open since M3.1 #6, M4 #24, M4.2 #13). Today crates/gear-cli/src/main.rs fn cmd_admin_stop sends SIGTERM and returns; the engine process has no handler, so it dies wherever it is, and a merge whose gate is running is left Executing (the next start turns it Unknown for the captain). Change crates/gear-cli/src/main.rs only, no other design: (1) the engine process (where `run_engine` is called for the real loop) installs a SIGTERM handler (`libc::signal` with an `extern "C" fn` that only stores true in a `static STOP: AtomicBool`); fn run_engine_with takes a `stop: &dyn Fn() -> bool` and checks it before each tick and once per second of the sleep between ticks (the sleep becomes `cfg.tick_s` one-second sleeps), leaving the loop when it is true, so a running tick, and the merge in it, always finishes; run_engine passes `&|| STOP.load(Ordering::SeqCst)`. (2) cmd_admin_stop, after the kill, waits until the engine lock can be taken (fn take_engine_lock, tried once a second, at most 20 minutes), printing once "waiting for the engine to finish its tick (a merge gate may be running)" when the first try fails, then "engine stopped (pid <pid>)"; past 20 minutes it says "the engine (pid <pid>) is still running after 20 min" and exits 1. Put the wait in `fn wait_for_lock(try_lock: &mut dyn FnMut() -> bool, sleep: &mut dyn FnMut(), tries: u32) -> bool` so tests inject both.
accept: CLI unit tests: run_engine_with with a tick that sets the stop flag during its own run returns after that tick, and that tick's report is written; with stop already true no tick runs; wait_for_lock returns true when try_lock succeeds on the third try (sleep called twice) and false after `tries` failures. No test sleeps on the wall clock. Run only `make check CRATE=gear-cli`; the merge gate runs `make gate`.

## T-267 [must] lane=hull milestone=M5 required
goal: [v0.5.0 S1] A reviewer's packet is confirmed by the reviewer's first `gear`, so a lost packet shows at once and the home count of unconfirmed deliveries is true (M5's backlog: "confirm a delivery by the agent's own first action"; seen again in M5's first hour: T-260's direction packet O-3422 went to a fresh Codex session that never saw it, and it and the three nudges after it, O-3424..O-3426, all stay "submitted" because a reviewer delivery is confirmed only by the verdict). Change, no other design: (1) crates/gear-engine/src/ops.rs, where a reviewer's Deliver op is checked (the `OpKind::Deliver if is_reviewer(…)` branch that calls fn review_verdict): it is Confirmed by the first view of the same seat and generation at or after the op's `created` (`tx.views_since(&op.seat, op.generation, op.created)`), with the evidence text `view <seat>/<generation> @ <ts>`, or, as today, by the attempt's review verdict, whichever came first; the newest one wins (hull's question M-4398): while a newer Submitted Deliver op of the same seat for the same task and attempt exists, an older one is not confirmed; when the newest is confirmed, every older Submitted one is set Void with the result "superseded by <op id>" (written directly, as release.rs does for a Submitted op), and a Confirmed op is never rewritten. ## T-275 [must] lane=mast milestone=M5 required
goal: [v0.5.1 S2] Each role's place is written plainly in the skill. Text changes only, exactly these, no other design: (1) skills/gear/references/captain.md, the paragraph under the title ("You are the captain of one GEAR project. You plan, decide and answer; you do not write or review code. Lanes build, …") becomes: "You are the captain of one GEAR project: the brain of the whole work. You think so that the lanes need not: you design, decide, write every contract so exactly that a lane implements it without thinking, and judge every finding against the goal. You write no product code, not even a one-line fix: every change to what the repository ships (its code, tests, build and the files lanes own) is a lane's task, and a hand fix is only operational (a store state, a restart, a seat freed), its root fix a task. Scripts for your own work are yours to write: reading the store, counting data, checking a log; they stay outside the repository. Lanes implement; temp reviewers bring what the lanes built into line with your intent; the engine delivers work, starts reviews, settles passing verdicts and merges. A captain that only passes messages on and acknowledges reports is not doing its work. How to keep all of it aimed at the goal: `gear --skill course`." (1b) skills/gear/references/captain.md, §1 Kickoff, after the step that writes the plan (find the step that names `# Queue` or the plan file): add the step "**Decompose the whole voyage before departure.** Every backlog item the kickoff takes becomes tasks in the plan now, each with its contract; a lane starts only when the whole voyage is in the queue. A cut that proves wrong is adjusted mid-voyage; the rest of the voyage is never written in waves, or work is dropped along the way." (2) skills/gear/references/rules/review.md, the sentence "You judge one candidate against its contract and the voyage's bar." becomes "You bring what the lane built into line with the captain's intent: you judge one candidate against its contract, the captain's intent written down, and the voyage's bar." (3) skills/gear/references/rules/lane.md line 3 "The captain plans and decides; you implement." becomes "The captain thinks, plans and decides; you implement what the packet says, without designing."
accept: The three texts read exactly as above (`gear --skill captain`, `gear --skill review` or the rules topic that prints review.md, and the lane rules print them); the skill tests that check topic texts pass. Run only `make check CRATE=gear-app`; the merge gate runs `make gate`.

## T-280 [must] lane=hull milestone=M5 required
goal: [v0.5.0 S1] A merge that loses the push race goes back to the queue instead of failing (several fleets on one repository race on one integration branch, several projects' merge workers push to it at once). Today crates/gear-engine/src/merge.rs fn merge_with_retries already re-runs fn merge_one_locked on a `Blocker::Clear` failure — every retry fetches with `--prune`, resets to `<remote>/<integration>`, merges the candidate and the review branch again and re-runs the gate — but a failure whose reason starts with `RACE` ("push race: ") counts against `MERGE_RETRIES` (3) like any other, and after the third the worker returns `Step::Failed`, which fn merge_passed turns into `fail_merge` (op Failed, a captain item, a `- remerge` by hand). Change crates/gear-engine/src/merge.rs only, no other design: (1) a new `Report::Raced(String)` and a new `Step::Raced(String)`; in fn merge_with_retries, when `pr` is None and the failure that would be returned (blocker Clear, `attempt >= MERGE_RETRIES`) has a reason that starts with `RACE` and contains "non-fast-forward" or "fetch first" (the remote moved), return `Step::Raced(reason)` instead of `Step::Failed`; a "cannot lock ref" race (it can be a stale local lock, not a move on the remote) keeps today's Failed end; the worker maps `Step::Raced(reason)` to `Report::Raced(reason)` where it maps the other steps, and the test helper fn step_result maps it to `Err(Failed::clear(reason))`; (2) fn merge_passed collects `Report::Raced(reason)` with `retry_merge(store, project, op, &format!("{} — the integration branch moved during each of {} tries; it merges again from the new tip on the next tick", <the first line of reason>, MERGE_RETRIES + 1), finished.at)` (op back to Unknown, so `ready_merges` runs it again next tick: fetch, merge, gate, push as on every run), no `fail_merge`, no captain item; (3) PR mode (fn pr_head's race at ~1973, `pr` Some) is unchanged. A race is never counted across ticks: each time it loses, another merge landed, so the queue moves on. Every other clear failure keeps today's three retries and its Failed end. The code change is in crates/gear-engine/src/merge.rs only; tests go where the crate keeps them.
accept: merge.rs tests with the fixtures of `a_push_race_is_retried_and_the_merge_lands`: a test runner like `FailOnceRemote` that refuses with "! [rejected] dev -> dev (non-fast-forward)" on its first 4 pushes makes `merge_with_retries` return `Step::Raced` whose reason starts with "push race: "; a merge_passed test driven by the existing `drive`/`ticks` helpers where the first launch races 4 times and the second launch's pushes succeed ends with the op Confirmed, no Failed state on the way and no message on the task's `failed_thread`; a runner that fails with "push failed: hook declined" 4 times still ends Failed with the captain item (today's path). Existing merge tests pass. Run only `make check CRATE=gear-engine`; the merge gate runs `make gate`.

The code change is in crates/gear-engine/src/review.rs only; tests go where the crate keeps them.

(3) crates/gear-cli/src/main.rs fn run_plan: after a successful apply whose draft has a `# Milestone` block, the text output ends with the line `commit the plan as: <plan_message filled with {project} = the draft's project and {milestone} = the id of the draft's last milestone block (gear_app::draft::milestone_parts), {what} left for the captain>`; the JSON output gains `"commit"` holding only the filled template, e.g. "M2: {what}" (data for a script, not the sentence; absent without a milestone block; mast's question M-4649); the template is read from the project's stored config (`gear_app::config::conventions_or_default`). ## T-302 [must] lane=mast milestone=M5 required
goal: [v0.5.0 S1] Several fleets on one repository (1)(2)(5): two projects on one repo cannot share a voyage folder, and two projects on one GitHub repo cannot take the same issues. Today crates/gear-app/src/admin.rs fn branch_clash refuses only branch templates without `{project}` (T-256); two projects whose `conventions.voyage_dir` expands to the same folder (the default `gear/voyages/{milestone}` has no `{project}`) overwrite each other's plan, reports and reviews, and two projects in GitHub mode on one `owner/name` with overlapping or empty `[github.issues] labels` both take the same issue. Change crates/gear-app/src/admin.rs fn branch_clash (its two callers, register/re_register through fn check_other_projects and the `--check` path of crates/gear-cli/src/main.rs, stay as they are), no other design. For each other project that parses, in this order: (a) the GitHub check (2) below, over every pair of repos, whatever the local paths; (b) no shared repository path (today's `repo_paths` match) → next project; (c) today's branch check: templates not all naming `{project}` → return today's branch text; (d) the voyage-folder check (1). The checks: (1) when `gear_model::layout::voyage_dir(&config.conventions.voyage_dir, &config.project.id, "{milestone}")` equals the same expansion of the other's template with its own id, return `project <other> also works in <path> and keeps its voyages in the same folder <that expansion>: put {project} in conventions.voyage_dir so the two projects' plans, reports and reviews cannot overwrite each other`; (2) for every pair of a repo of this config and a repo of the other (`ProjectConfig::repos()`) that both have `github: Some(g)` with the same `g.repo` (compared ASCII case-insensitively): when either side's `issues_labels` is empty return `project <other> also takes issues from GitHub <repo>: each project needs its own non-empty [github.issues] labels`; when the two label lists share a label return `project <other> also takes issues labelled <shared labels joined by ", "> from GitHub <repo>: give each project labels of its own`. The doc comment of fn branch_clash says it checks branches, voyage folders and GitHub labels. The code change is in admin.rs only; tests go where the crates keep them.

## T-303 [must] lane=mast milestone=M5 required
goal: [v0.5.0 S1] `- release <reviewer>` takes a reviewer whose agent is gone even while it holds a task, and a staff line that does nothing says so (another project's captain, 2026-10-28; M4.2 T-245: "`- release review-11` applied with no error and no effect"). Today crates/gear-app/src/plan.rs fn release_reviewer refuses every seat with a `task_id` ("<name> is reviewing T-n"), so a dead reviewing seat could be cleared only by an unreviewed verdict (the engine now frees it by itself, T-264; this is the captain's own way out), and the lane staff loop of fn apply_queue skips a line whose target is already reached (`seat.state == target`) without a word. Change, no other design: (1) fn release_seat passes `agents` to fn release_reviewer. For a reviewer with a task: when `agents` is Some and the seat's binding at its generation is Active and no agent in `agents` has the binding's `pane_id` and `kind` (the test fn release_lane makes), clear the seat's task (`tx.clear_seat_task`) and set it `Releasing` with the reason "released by the captain: its agent is gone mid-review"; the task stays Verifying with its attempt handed in, and the engine's normal start_reviews pass starts the review again. When its agent is there, refuse as today ("<name> is reviewing <task>"); when `agents` is None, refuse with "<name> is reviewing <task> (the session's agents were not read, so GEAR cannot tell whether its agent is gone)". A reviewer without a task behaves as today. (2) In the lane staff loop, where `seat.state == target` records the decision and continues, also push to `warnings` the line "- <verb> <lane>: the lane is already <state>, nothing to do" (verb park/hold/retire, state its `as_str()`). The code change is in crates/gear-app/src/plan.rs only; tests go where the crate keeps them.

## T-304 [must] lane=mast milestone=M5 required
goal: [v0.5.0 S1] The lane quota counts the lanes that can run, not every lane in the config (M3.1 B02-01, postponed 4 voyages). Today crates/gear-app/src/config.rs fn validate refuses `cfg.lanes.len() > cfg.quota.lanes` ("{n} lanes are configured but the quota allows {q}"), so a retired lane that stays listed takes a slot; and crates/gear-app/src/plan.rs fn apply_queue brings a retired lane back when a Ready task names it (T-247) without looking at the quota. Decision: the quota counts every lane seat that is not Retired, and a config lane that has no seat yet. Change, no other design: (1) config.rs fn validate drops the `quota.lanes` check (it cannot see the seats). (2) crates/gear-app/src/admin.rs: `pub fn lane_quota_problem(config: &config::ProjectConfig, retired: &[String]) -> Option<String>` returns `quota.lanes: <n> lanes would run but the quota allows <q> (a retired lane does not count)` when the config's lanes whose name is not in `retired` number more than `config.quota.lanes`; fn register calls it with no retired lanes and fn re_register with the names of this project's Lane seats in `SeatState::Retired`, each refusing with `ErrorCode::BadFormat` and that text before anything is written. (3) crates/gear-cli/src/main.rs, the `--check` path of fn cmd_admin_init: after the branch check, call it with the retired lane names read from the same store `other_projects` reads (a new `fn retired_lanes_of(project: &str) -> GearResult<Vec<String>>` beside it, the same path rule, read-only, empty without a store) and fail with BadFormat when it returns a problem. (4) plan.rs, the revival loop of fn apply_queue (the `"a task names it again"` block): before setting a Retired seat Queued, count the project's Lane seats not Retired; when that count is already `project.quota_lanes` or more, leave it Retired and push the warning "lane <name> stays retired: <count> lanes run and the quota allows <q>; retire another lane or raise [quota] lanes". The code change is in config.rs, admin.rs, plan.rs and crates/gear-cli/src/main.rs only; tests go where the crates keep them.

## T-305 [must] lane=mast milestone=M5 required
goal: [v0.5.0 S1] A rebind says what it changes, a pending one can be withdrawn, and an applied one says how to undo it; the captain's guide says to rebind an idle lane (M3.1 B02-01, M4 #1). Today crates/gear-app/src/plan.rs fn apply_queue prints only "rebind <lane>: applied" or "rebind <lane>: waits for T-…" for `- rebind <lane> repo=<name> owns=[…]` (fn request_rebind), a recorded request (meta `rebind_pending_key`) has no way back except a second rebind, and skills/gear/references/captain.md (the `# Staff` line) has no rule for an idle lane. Decision: the preview is the apply line itself (old and new values; a waiting rebind changes nothing until its tasks settle), the rollback of a pending rebind is `- rebind <lane> cancel`, and an applied one is undone by the rebind line its apply prints. Change, no other design: (1) crates/gear-app/src/draft.rs: a `StaffItem::RebindCancel { line: usize, lane: String }`, parsed from `- rebind <lane> cancel`; fn staff_line, fn staff_text (`rebind <lane> cancel`) and the staff loop's match in plan.rs know it like `Rebind`. (2) plan.rs, the rebind loop at the top of fn apply_queue: the message becomes `rebind <lane>: repo <old repo name, "root" for ""> → <new>, owns [<old owns>] → [<new owns>]: applied (to undo: - rebind <lane> repo=<old> owns=[<old owns>])`, the undo part left out when the old repo is the root (fn rebind_config takes only a `[[repos]]` name) or `…: waits for <tasks>; nothing changes until then (to withdraw: - rebind <lane> cancel)`, the old values read from the project's stored config (`config.repo_of_lane(lane).name` and the lane's `owns`) before the request; a `RebindCancel` sets `rebind_pending_key(<seat>)` to "" and warns `rebind <lane>: the pending rebind is withdrawn`, warns `rebind <lane>: nothing pending to withdraw` when there is none, and is refused with "lane <lane> is still moving to its new worktree" while `lane-rebind-applied:<seat>` is set; each is recorded as a staff decision like `Rebind`. (3) skills/gear/references/captain.md, the `# Staff` fields line: list `- rebind <lane> cancel`, and add: "A lane whose repo has no work left while another repo has queued work is rebound at once, not left idle; the apply line says what changes and how to undo it." The code change is in draft.rs, plan.rs and captain.md only; tests go where the crate keeps them.
accept: (Moved to mast at M-4870 so mast has startable work while the board chain waits for merges: the files this task names are allowed edits for mast.)  Plan tests (the existing rebind tests' fixtures): an idle lane's rebind result names the old and the new repo and owns and the undo line; a rebind that waits names its tasks and the withdraw line, `- rebind <lane> cancel` in the next apply clears the pending request so the engine's `reconcile_rebinds` applies nothing after the task settles; a cancel with nothing pending warns; a draft test parses `- rebind hull cancel`. Run only `make check CRATE=gear-app`; the merge gate runs `make gate`.

## T-306 [must] lane=mast milestone=M5 required
goal: [v0.5.0 S1] `- remerge` of a candidate that is already on the integration branch queues nothing and settles its failed-merge item (T-92 F1, open since M4 #9). Today crates/gear-app/src/plan.rs fn remerge checks only that the task's last merge op Failed and queues a new merge op; a candidate that landed by another way (a hand merge, another task that carried it) is merged "again", and if the captain does not remerge, the engine's `merge-failed:<task>` item stays open forever. Change, no other design: (1) before the write transaction, beside `cancellable_done_tasks`, a new `fn remerges_already_merged(store: &mut Store, draft: &Draft) -> GearResult<BTreeSet<String>>`: for each `StaffItem::Remerge { task, .. }`, the candidate of the task's last Accepted attempt, and, only when the project's stored config text parses (`crate::config::parse`, skipped when empty), `on_integration(&config, task.lane.as_deref(), &candidate)`; `Ok(true)` puts the task in the set, `Ok(false)` and `Err(_)` do not (a git failure leaves the remerge as today: the engine's merge confirms an already-merged candidate itself). (2) apply_queue takes the set as `cancellable` is taken; fn remerge gets `already: bool` and returns `GearResult<Option<String>>`: after the last-op checks, when `already` is true it inserts no op, resolves the task's open or waiting items of the threads `merge-failed:<task>` and `push:<task>` (fn resolve_merge_items, which gets the result text as a parameter: "cancelled by the captain" for the cancel path as today, "the candidate <sha> is already on the integration branch" here) and returns `Some("remerge <task>: <sha> is already on the integration branch: no merge queued, its failed-merge item resolved")`, which the staff loop pushes to `warnings`; otherwise it queues the op as today and returns None. The code change is in crates/gear-app/src/plan.rs only; tests go where the crate keeps them.
accept: (Moved to mast at M-4870 so mast has startable work while the board chain waits for merges: the files this task names are allowed edits for mast.)  Tests in crates/gear-app/tests/it/remerge_contract.rs, with a git fixture built once per file (a bare origin and a clone, as crates/gear-app/tests/it/cancel_contract.rs builds them; the project's config names the clone as its repo): a task whose last merge Failed and whose accepted candidate is on origin/dev gets no new merge op, its open `merge-failed:T-1` item is resolved with the text above and the apply warns; the same with the candidate not on origin/dev queues the op as today; the existing remerge tests (config without a readable repo) pass unchanged. Run only `make check CRATE=gear-app`; the merge gate runs `make gate`.

Today crates/gear-engine/src/opencode.rs fn derived_labels returns `None` both when OpenCode's `model.list` answers without the model and when the call fails, its data is not an array, or the list is empty, and a failed `provider.list` falls back to the provider id, a label no status bar shows; crates/gear-engine/src/switch.rs fn ensure_model then fails the seat with "… is not in OpenCode's model list", sending the captain after a model id that is right. (3) fn plan_target uses only `Ok(Some(labels))`, as it uses `Some` today. The code change is in crates/gear-engine/src/opencode.rs and crates/gear-engine/src/switch.rs only; tests go where the crate keeps them.

## T-308 [must] lane=mast milestone=M5 required
goal: [v0.5.0 S1] A reset time read from a provider's own limit text, for providers GEAR has no quota reader for (M4 #20; M4.2: "No quota reader for TokenRhythm: its stop is known only from errors"). Today gear-usage reads resets only through the minimax, opencode-go and codex readers (crates/gear-usage/src/quota.rs), and every other provider is parked for the fixed `[models] quota_reset_h`. Decision: no new API readers (none is documented for the providers in use); the reset is read from the text the agent shows, when that text states it. Change, no other design: `pub fn reset_from_text(text: &str, now: Ts) -> Option<Ts>`, exported in lib.rs beside `read_quota`. It reads the lines from the last to the first and returns the first match, considering only a line that contains (ASCII case-insensitively) "reset", "try again", "retry" or "available again": (1) an RFC 3339 timestamp among its whitespace-separated words, trailing `.,;)` trimmed (the crate's fn rfc3339) → that time; (2) `retry-after: <n>` → now + n seconds; (3) `in <duration>` or `after <duration>`, where duration is one or more `<number><unit>` groups with or without spaces between number and unit (`2h 13m`, `45 minutes`, `3 hours 5 min`, `90s`), units d/day/days, h/hr/hrs/hour/hours, m/min/mins/minute/minutes, s/sec/secs/second/seconds → now + the sum. A result not after `now`, or more than 8 days after it, is None for the whole call: the last matching line is the provider's latest word, and an earlier line may be stale (mast's question M-4692); anything else (a clock time such as "resets 3pm", whose time zone GEAR does not know) is None. The code change is in crates/gear-usage/src/quota.rs and crates/gear-usage/src/lib.rs only; tests go where the crate keeps them.
accept: (Moved to mast for balance at station M-4643: the files this task names are allowed edits for mast.)  Unit tests in crates/gear-usage/tests/it/quota_contract.rs: "Usage limit reached. Try again in 2h 13m." → now + 7980; "rate limit, resets in 45 minutes" → now + 2700; "Retry-After: 120" → now + 120; "quota resets at 2026-10-29T05:00:00Z." → that time; "resets 3pm" → None; a matching line followed below by an unrelated line still matches; "in 2h" on a line without a reset word → None; "resets in 9 days" → None. Run only `make check CRATE=gear-usage`; the merge gate runs `make gate`.
estimate: 25

## T-311 [must] lane=mast milestone=M5 required
goal: [v0.5.0 S1] Moving a running task to another lane in the plan moves its work (M4.2 #10: M4 20:27, T-160 keel → hull; the captain abandoned attempt 1 in the store). Today crates/gear-app/src/plan.rs fn apply_queue, for an existing task, sets `task.lane = item.lane.clone()` and nothing else, so the open attempt stays on the old seat, the old lane keeps working on it and the new lane never gets it. Change, no other design: (1) the attempt loop of fn cancel_task becomes `fn abandon_open_attempts(tx, project, task_id, now) -> GearResult<bool>` (same rules: Unknown → Stopped → Abandoned, any other non-terminal attempt → Abandoned, a refused transition is BadTransition), used by cancel_task as today. (2) In the existing-task branch, when `item.lane` differs from the task's lane, the rule is keyed on the task's attempts, not on its state (a rework attempt n+1 is opened while the task is Ready): an attempt in Submitted (handed in, under review) refuses the change with `bad(item.line, "<task> is handed in (attempt <n>) and under review: change its lane after the verdict")`; otherwise every attempt with `state.occupies_seat()` (Delivered, Started, Stopped, Unknown) is closed by `abandon_open_attempts`, and when one was closed the task goes Ready (from Running or Waiting; a Ready task stays Ready), its wait is cleared as the contract-change path clears it, and the old lane's seat, when it exists, gets one Instruction, thread `<task>:moved`, body "<task> moved to lane <new>; stop work on it, nothing to hand in"; a task with no such attempt only changes its lane, as today. A block that also cancels the task (`state: cancelled`) skips this move rule (cancel wins), and a block without `lane=` is not a move (mast's question M-4919). The decision log records `<task> moved <old> → <new>` (fn record_gear_decision, kind "staff"). The code change is in crates/gear-app/src/plan.rs only; tests go where the crate keeps them.
accept: (Moved to mast at M-4870 so mast has startable work while the board chain waits for merges: the files this task names are allowed edits for mast.)  Tests in crates/gear-app/tests/it/cancel_contract.rs (its fixtures): a started task moved from heat to cold has its attempt Abandoned, is Ready on cold and heat has the "moved" instruction; a delivered (not started) attempt is abandoned too; a rework attempt 2 opened after a fail verdict (task Ready) is abandoned and the new lane gets the task; a handed-in task's move is refused and nothing is written; a Ready task with no attempt moves with no message; the cancel tests pass unchanged. Run only `make check CRATE=gear-app`; the merge gate runs `make gate`.

## T-315 [must] lane=mast milestone=M5 required
goal: [v0.5.0 S1] `gear admin init` and `--check` say when a configured model id is not one its CLI lists (M4.2 #17 (2): a wrong model id pinned a review round for good). Today only claude ids get a `/`-prefix check (T-235); crates/gear-app/src/kickoff.rs already knows how each CLI lists its models (fn models_of: `opencode models`, `pi --list-models`, the Codex cache) but init never asks it. Decision: a warning, not a refusal (a list can be not loaded yet or differ by machine; B8), and only on request, `gear admin init --check --models`, since asking the CLIs runs them (`opencode models` waits 500 ms when its list is empty) and the plain `--check` and init stay offline and fast. Change, no other design: (1) kickoff.rs: fn models_of becomes `pub`; a new `pub fn model_warnings(config: &crate::config::ProjectConfig, listed: &mut dyn FnMut(&str) -> (Vec<String>, Option<String>)) -> Vec<String>`: for every lane's chain (`config::lane_chain`), the review chain with `[temps] kind` (skipped without `[temps]`), and the captain chain with `captain.kind`, each entry's CLI is `entry.cli()` or the seat's kind; `listed` is called once per CLI; when it returns models and the entry's `model()` is not among them, the line `lane <name>: model <m> is not among the models <cli> lists` (`reviewers:` / `captain:` for those chains); when it returns no models and a note, one line per CLI `model ids of <cli> not checked: <note>`; no models and no note (a CLI that prints none) says nothing. (2) crates/gear-cli/src/main.rs: `AdminOp::Init` gains `#[arg(long, requires = "check")] models: bool`; fn cmd_admin_init, with `--check --models`, after the seat lines prints each warning to stderr as `warning: <line>`, with `gear_app::kickoff::models_of` as `listed`, and the JSON gains `"model_warnings": [...]`; without `--models` nothing runs. Exit codes are unchanged. (3) skills/gear/references/captain.md, §1 Kickoff, the step that checks the config with `gear admin init --check gear/gear.toml`: it says `gear admin init --check --models gear/gear.toml` and "a model warning is fixed before departure". The code change is in kickoff.rs, main.rs and captain.md only; tests go where the crates keep them.

A CLI test runs `--check --models` with PATH set to a scratch dir holding only a fake `pi` script that prints `provider model context` and `tok deep-1 600K`: a lane of kind pi on `tok/deep-2` gives the warning on stderr and exit 0; plain `--check` on the same config runs no `pi` (the script writes a marker file that stays absent). ## T-316 [must] lane=mast milestone=M5 required
goal: [v0.5.0 S1] A test fixture never registers into the user's store (M4.2 #35: the user's board showed a leftover fixture project with empty tokens, its captain seat bound to the real captain's pane; the fixture rows were removed by hand). The code change is in fn cmd_admin_init of crates/gear-cli/src/main.rs only; tests go where the crate keeps them.

## T-317 [must] lane=mast milestone=M5 required
goal: [v0.5.0 S1] A board opened without a project shows the session's projects one at a time with a key to switch (M4.2 #35; T-249 added `gear board --project <id>`). The store is per Herdr session since v0.4.5, so its projects are the session's; but crates/gear-board/src/frame.rs draws only `board.projects.first()`, so with no `--project` and no seat the board shows the first project and the others cannot be reached. Change, no other design: (1) run_with keeps `shown: usize` (0 at start); the board it builds each frame becomes `let mut board`; after each build, when `opts.project` is None and the board holds more than one project, `shown %= board.projects.len()` and `board.projects.rotate_left(shown)`, so the frame draws the shown one; the key `p` adds one to `shown`, resets `selected` and the panel, and redraws. (2) fn header: when `board.projects.len() > 1`, the title row ends with ` · p: next of <n> projects`. A board for one project (with `--project`, a seat, or a store of one project) is unchanged, and so are the JSON and the printed-once output. The S4 board tasks keep this suffix and key. The code change is in fn run_with of crates/gear-board/src/lib.rs and fn header of crates/gear-board/src/frame.rs only; tests go where the crate keeps them.

## T-320 [must] lane=mast milestone=M5 required
goal: [v0.5.0 S1] An instruction the captain needs confirmed is acknowledged (T-181, cut from M4; design §10.7 row B7). Today `gear send --kind instruction --to <lane>` stores an Open message to the lane and the captain never learns whether it was read; MsgKind::Ack exists but crates/gear-app/src/mail.rs fn send_inner refuses it ("kind ack is not available yet"), and `NewMessage.needs_ack` is always false there. Change crates/gear-app/src/mail.rs, crates/gear-cli/src/main.rs and skills/gear/references/captain.md only, no other design: (1) mail.rs: `pub fn send_with_ack(store, env, fp, args, now)` calls send_inner with a new `ack: bool` (true; fn send and fn send_direction pass false), which request_key also takes, as it takes `direction`. With `ack`: the message must be kind Instruction from the captain with `--to`, else BadFormat "`--ack` belongs to a captain's instruction with `--to`"; it is stored with `needs_ack: true`, its body then ends (tx.update_message_body) with the line `Acknowledge it: gear send --kind ack --reply-to <its id> "<what you will do>"`, and the captain gets an item: thread `ack:<its id>`, sender and recipient the captain, kind System, body `<seat name> has not acknowledged <its id> yet: <first 60 characters of the instruction>`, inserted Open and set Waiting with `wait_until = now + 600` (tx.update_message_state), so it comes back into the inbox after 10 minutes. (2) `MsgKind::Ack` is accepted with `--reply-to` only: the original must be an Instruction with `needs_ack` whose recipient is the caller's seat, else BadFormat "an ack answers an instruction that asks for one: --reply-to M-<n>"; it is stored as Info to the original's sender, and in the same request the open or waiting items of thread `ack:<original id>` are Resolved with the result "acknowledged in <ack id>". (3) main.rs SendCmd gains `#[arg(long)] ack: bool`; fn run_send calls send_with_ack when it is set. (4) captain.md, §Answer (the paragraph that starts "**Answer** each item"): add "An instruction you need confirmed goes with `--ack`: your item stays until the lane acknowledges it, and comes back after 10 minutes." This is the receipt of S2's "helping" work (batch C): a lane holding an unacknowledged instruction is what C1 shows. The code change is in mail.rs, main.rs and captain.md only; tests go where the crates keep them.

Run only `make check CRATE=gear-app` and `make check CRATE=gear-cli`; the merge gate runs `make gate`.

## T-323 [must] lane=mast milestone=M5 required
goal: [v0.5.1 S2] A lane that is helping on a captain's instruction is shown as helping, not as idle (M5 kickoff decision, S2 "work that is not a task": a lane that holds an unanswered captain instruction counts as busy, and the board and home show "helping <lane>: …"; its reply ends it; trial feedback B04-01: a lane giving read-only evidence to another lane looked idle and drew IDLE and stall alerts). Today nothing in crates/gear-board/src/idle.rs fn idle_reason, crates/gear-board/src/lib.rs fn lane_line or crates/gear-board/src/frame.rs fn view_now knows about an instruction: a lane with no open attempt is "no current task" or "idle: nothing left planned". This task adds one predicate that the engine half (the next hull task) uses too. Change, no other design: (1) crates/gear-board/src/idle.rs: `pub const HELP_LIMIT_S: i64 = 1800;`, `pub struct Helping { pub message: String, pub lane: Option<String>, pub words: String, pub since: Ts }` with `pub fn text(&self) -> String` = `helping <lane>: <words>` when `lane` is Some, else `helping the captain: <words>`, and `pub fn helping(tx: &Tx<'_>, project: &str, seat: &SeatRow, now: Ts) -> GearResult<Option<Helping>>`: None unless `seat.role == SeatRole::Lane`, `tx.in_progress_attempts_of_seat(&seat.id)?` is empty and the project has a captain (`tx.captain_of(project)?`); then, among `tx.messages_to_since(project, &seat.id, now - HELP_LIMIT_S)?` whose kind is `MsgKind::Instruction` and whose sender is the captain's seat id, newest first, the first one that has no reply — no message in `tx.messages_from_since(project, &seat.id, instruction.created)?` with `reply_to == Some(instruction.id)` — gives `Helping { message: its id, lane, words, since: its created }`, where `lane` is the lane of the instruction's task (`instruction.task` → `tx.get_task(project, …)?` → `.lane`) when that lane is not `seat.name`, else None, and `words` is the first 6 whitespace-separated words of the body joined by one space, followed by " …" when the body has more. An instruction older than HELP_LIMIT_S no longer counts, so a lane that never answers comes back to the normal idle and stall rules. (2) fn idle_reason: right after the "The lane works" check (`if is_working(tx, &seat_id)? { return Ok(None); }`), return Ok(None) when the seat exists and `helping(tx, project, &seat, now)?` is Some. (3) crates/gear-board/src/lib.rs: `LaneBoard` gains `pub helping: Option<String>` (None in the two places that build a LaneBoard), filled in the loop that sets `lane.idle_text` (the one that calls `idle::idle_reason`) with `idle::helping(…)?.map(|h| h.text())` for the lane's seat `<project>:<lane>`; fn lane_line uses that text in place of "no current task" when the lane has no current task and `helping` is Some. (4) crates/gear-board/src/frame.rs fn view_now: a lane with `helping` Some and no current task shows that text in the middle column with `Style::fg(Color::Acc)` (before the `idle_text` arm). (5) crates/gear-app/src/plan.rs fn idle_lanes skips a lane seat for which `gear_board::idle::helping(tx, project, &seat, now)?` is Some (an allowed edit). The home page needs no change: its lane lines come from fn lane_line. The code change is in crates/gear-board/src/idle.rs, lib.rs, frame.rs and crates/gear-app/src/plan.rs only; tests go where the crates keep them.
accept: Board tests (crates/gear-board/tests/it/idle_contract.rs, its fixtures): a lane with no attempt and a captain Instruction to it about T-2 of lane mast (body "Read the log of T-2 and send mast the failing line please") gives `helping` Some with text "helping mast: Read the log of T-2 and …", `idle_reason` None and `lane_line` containing "helping mast: Read the log"; the same instruction without a task gives "helping the captain: …"; after the lane sends a message with `reply_to` that instruction's id, `helping` is None and the idle reason is back; an instruction created 1801 s before `now` gives None; an instruction from the engine (sender "engine") gives None; a lane with a Started attempt gives None. A gear-app test: `plan::idle_lanes` does not list a helping lane. Existing board and home tests pass. Run only `make check CRATE=gear-board` and `make check CRATE=gear-app`; the merge gate runs `make gate`.

## T-324 [must] lane=hull milestone=M5 required
goal: [v0.5.1 S2] The engine raises no IDLE, run-dry, busy or stall item for a lane that is helping on a captain's instruction (the engine half of "work that is not a task"; the board half adds `gear_board::idle::helping`). (2) busy.rs fn check: a lane for which `gear_board::idle::helping(tx, project, &seat, now)?` is Some does not qualify (it goes the `recover` way, as any lane that does not qualify). The code change is in crates/gear-engine/src/feed.rs, busy.rs and lib.rs only; tests go where the crate keeps them.

## T-326 [must] lane=hull milestone=M5 required
goal: [v0.5.1 S2] With `on_wait = "release"`, a lane parked on a long quota wait has its agent exit, so it uses nothing until the reset. Today crates/gear-engine/src/quota.rs parks the seat (fn park_for_quota, fn park_unreadable, and fn park for a policy park) and the agent stays in its pane until fn wake_from_source or the `parked_until` branch continues it. The pane is kept as a shell (not closed), so the comeback can start the same CLI in the same pane and worktree with its session resumed through crate::restart (the next hull task). Change, no other design: in fn apply_quota_policy_with, inside the `if seat.state == SeatState::Parked` branch and before the wake checks, call a new `fn release_for_wait(store, herdr, project, &config, profile, seat, now) -> GearResult<Option<String>>` and, when it returns Some(line), push the line and `continue`. It releases only when all hold: `config.models.on_wait == WaitPolicy::Release`; `seat.role == SeatRole::Lane`; the meta `meta_key("wait_released", &seat.id)` is empty or missing; the wait ends at least `RELEASE_MIN_WAIT_S` (a new const, 900) after now — the wait is `load_wait(store, &seat.id)?` with `pause` true (a quota or unreadable park; the 5-min provider-error wait is never released), else `parked_until(store, &seat.id)?`; `profile.resume_args.is_some()` and `profile.session_ref` (a CLI whose session cannot be resumed keeps its agent: the line says "<seat>: kept through the wait: its CLI cannot resume a session"); the seat's binding (`tx.get_binding(&seat.id, seat.generation)?`) is Active and its pane holds an agent (`herdr.agent_get(&pane)` is `Ok(Some(_))`). The release sends the exit the way crate::restart's stop phase does: `crate::names::ensure(herdr, &pane, &crate::names::desired(seat))`, then `profile.exit_command` as a prompt to `crate::names::live_name(herdr, &pane)` when it is set, else `herdr.send_keys(&pane, &profile.exit_keys)`; on `Outcome::Ok` or `Outcome::Uncertain` it sets the meta `meta_key("wait_released", &seat.id)` to "1" and tells the captain with `tell_captain` "<seat>: released for the wait until <until> (on_wait = release): its agent exited; its pane, worktree and session are kept"; on `Outcome::Refused` it sets nothing and the next tick tries again. The seat stays Parked and its binding Active; nothing else changes. With `on_wait = "keep"` (the default) the park is exactly as today. The code change is in crates/gear-engine/src/quota.rs only; tests go where the crate keeps them.
accept: quota.rs tests with the fixtures of `a_spent_window_parks_the_lane_until_the_reset` (source_world, tick_source, FakeHerdr, a config with `on_wait = "release"`): after the park, the next tick sends the profile's exit keys once (the fake's calls), sets the released meta and writes the captain's line; the seat stays Parked; a third tick sends nothing more; with `on_wait = "keep"` no exit is sent; a wait that ends 600 s after now is not released; a policy park (no source, `parked_until`) 2 h long is released; a profile without resume_args keeps its agent. Run only `make check CRATE=gear-engine TESTS='<a nextest filter: the tests of the module you changed and your new test>'`; the merge gate runs `make gate`.

## T-327 [must] lane=hull milestone=M5 required
goal: [v0.5.1 S2] A lane released for a quota wait comes back at the reset in the same pane and worktree with its session resumed and is told to continue. After the release task, a released lane is Parked, its binding Active and its pane a shell; today's wake paths in crates/gear-engine/src/quota.rs (fn wake_from_source types a Continue into the pane; the `parked_until` branch calls fn change_model, which switches a live agent in place for OpenCode) need a live agent. crate::restart already starts the same CLI in the same pane with the round's session resumed (fn advance: same-CLI plan with `resume` from `crate::session::round_session`, phase Stop sees the agent gone and goes to Start; fn finish sets the seat Active, records `engine.session_step` as a resume and delivers the Continue line of `crate::quota::resume_delivery`). Change, no other design: in the `if seat.state == SeatState::Parked` branch of fn apply_quota_policy_with, when the meta `meta_key("wait_released", &seat.id)` is "1" and the wait is over (the source wait of `load_wait` with `until <= now`, else `parked_until(store, &seat.id)?` with `until <= now`), call a new `fn bring_back(store, herdr, cfg, profiles, project, &config, seat, now) -> GearResult<Option<String>>` instead of fn wake_from_source and fn change_model, push its line when Some, and `continue`. fn bring_back: (1) in one write, for a source wait with `pause` true, ends the open `quota` pause and adds the round pause exactly as fn wake_from_source does (factor those writes into a helper both call); clears the wait meta (`wait_meta_key`), `quota_meta_key`, `meta_key("quota_error_ticks", …)`, `meta_key("resume", …)` and `meta_key("wait_released", …)`; (2) builds the target entry as fn restart_missing_jobs in crates/gear-engine/src/lib.rs builds it for a lane: the first entry of `crate::model::role_chain(&config, &seat.name)`, as `gear_app::config::ModelEntry::Entry(gear_app::config::ModelSpec { model, cli: Some(entry.cli() or the lane's kind), args: entry.args().to_vec(), context_window_k: None })`; an empty chain flags the seat with `flag_and_alert(store, project, seat, "quota: the lane's chain is empty; no model to bring it back on", now)`; (3) calls `crate::restart::advance(store, herdr, cfg, profiles, project, seat, Some(&entry), now)` and returns its line; a restart that is still pending continues on later ticks through the existing `crate::restart::pending` check at the top of the loop. A Parked seat without the released meta wakes exactly as today. The code change is in crates/gear-engine/src/quota.rs only; tests go where the crate keeps them.

## T-328 [must] lane=mast milestone=M5 required
goal: [v0.5.1 S2] A caller whose tools run in a daemon proves it is the seat's own session, not just any process outside the panes (T-123, moved to M5 by the user; gear/voyages/M4/backlog.md: "one OpenCode lane can pose as another"). Today crates/gear-app/src/identity.rs fn observe lets a caller whose ancestry reaches no pane keep its GEAR_CTX claim whenever the claimed pane's agent is a daemon kind (`real.is_none() && tools_outside_pane`, `TOOLS_OUTSIDE_PANE = ["opencode", "codex"]`), so any OpenCode tool shell of the same `opencode serve` can claim another OpenCode lane's seat. The fact each daemon gives its tool shells (read from the installed binaries, 2026-10-29): OpenCode's bash tool sets `OPENCODE_SESSION_ID` to the session id of the tool call (`ee.env.OPENCODE_SESSION_ID=Q.sessionID` in the opencode binary), the id GEAR records for an OpenCode seat when it starts it through the API; Codex lists `CODEX_THREAD_ID` among its exec environment variables. Change, no other design: (1) identity.rs: `#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default, PartialEq, Eq)] pub struct DaemonSessions { pub opencode: Option<String>, pub codex: Option<String> }` with `pub fn from_vars(vars: impl Fn(&str) -> Option<String>) -> DaemonSessions` reading `OPENCODE_SESSION_ID` and `CODEX_THREAD_ID` (an empty value is None). (2) `pub fn observe_via(env, herdr, ancestors) -> GearResult<Option<(Fingerprint, bool)>>` holds today's body of fn observe; the bool is true exactly when the daemon branch returned; fn observe becomes `observe_via(…).map(|found| found.map(|(fp, _)| fp))`. (3) fn prove_seat gains the parameter `daemon: &DaemonSessions` (after `ancestors`) and calls observe_via; when the daemon branch was taken and `env.ctx` is Some, it checks the session in a store read: the seat's known sessions are its binding's `session_ref` (`tx.get_binding(&seat_id, ctx.generation)?`), the fingerprint's `session_ref` and the `sessions` of `tx.open_round_of_seat(&seat_id)?` (empty values dropped); no known session → pass (nothing to prove against, as today); kind "opencode": `daemon.opencode` None → refuse with `NotRegistered` "seat <seat> is claimed from outside every pane without OPENCODE_SESSION_ID: an OpenCode tool shell always carries it, so this caller is not the seat's OpenCode session"; kind "codex": `daemon.codex` None → pass; an OpenCode value that is not one of the known sessions → refuse with `NotRegistered` "seat <seat> is claimed by OpenCode session <value>, but the seat's sessions are <known, joined by \", \">: a caller may act only as the seat of its own session"; a Codex value that is not one of the known sessions passes (that Codex's thread id equals the session GEAR records is not yet seen live, and this voyage's reviewers are Codex) and writes one captain message — thread `identity-codex:<seat>:<generation>`, kind `MsgKind::System`, state `MsgState::Info`, not urgent, body "seat <seat> was used by Codex thread <value>, while the seat's sessions are <known>: if this repeats, a Codex agent may be acting as another seat", skipped when that thread already holds a message. A refusal is reported with fn report_claim, as the other refusals of fn prove_seat are. (4) crates/gear-cli/src/main.rs fn observe_identity passes `&gear_app::identity::DaemonSessions::from_vars(|name| std::env::var(name).ok())`. The code change is in crates/gear-app/src/identity.rs and crates/gear-cli/src/main.rs only; tests go where the crates keep them.
accept: gear-app identity tests (crates/gear-app/tests/it/app_identity.rs, the fixtures of the T-126 daemon test: an OpenCode lane seat whose binding session_ref is "ses_a", the service ancestry): `OPENCODE_SESSION_ID=ses_a` passes; `ses_b` (another lane's session) is refused with the text above and writes one urgent captain item; no OPENCODE_SESSION_ID is refused; a seat with no known session passes without the variable; a Codex seat with session "th_1": `CODEX_THREAD_ID=th_1` passes, `th_2` passes and writes one Info message (a second call writes no second one), none passes; a caller inside its pane (ancestry matches) is unaffected. The existing prove_seat tests pass with `&DaemonSessions::default()`. Run only `make check CRATE=gear-app` and `make check CRATE=gear-cli`; the merge gate runs `make gate`.
